BBC A TAIWAN-UFO= (osfis} i
UFO spotted over central Taiwan town

An unidentified flying object (UFO) was spotted over central Taiwan
late last night, the Taiwanese news agency CNA reported, quoting
"eyewitnesses".

It said that the UFO had attracted a crowd of over 50 people as
it "floated" above a private house in Touliu around midnight local
time.

According to the eyewitnesses, the object, shaped like a bright
circle, occasinally jumped vertically and sometimes moved
horizontally. They said that "it did not resemble an aeroplane," the
agency reported.

LOOSE MINUTE

D/Sec(AS)/12/4
4 May 95

APS/US of S

D/USofS/0H/1407/95; GERRY SUTCLIFFE, MP - “UFOs"

1. Mr Sutcliffe's constituent, MOM, is a regular
correspondent on the subject of unidentified flying objects and

has written on five occasions over the last four months. The
detailed replies he has received to each of his letters very
clearly sets out the MOD's specific and limited role with
respect to "UFO" sightings. RESUME, however, refuses to
accept our explanation.

2. The alleged "UFO" research operation called "Aeneid" is
not recognized by this office or others, under this or any
other name, and the MOD certainly does not send "spies" to
"UFO" Conferences. The matters raised concerning the United
States and South Africa are not for us to comment upon.

3. I attach a draft response.

cng Sarees

Sec(AS}2

MB8247

D/US of S/0H/1407/95 May 1995

Thank you for your letter to Malcolm Rifkind of 13 April

regarding an enquiry you received from RSRiz® of
Section 40 Bradford, concerning unidentified flying

ebjects. I have been asked to reply.

has written to the MOD on a number of occasions
recently on this subject and my officials have replied to each
of his letters. These responses have explained the specific
and limited role and responsibilities my Department has in
connection with reports of "unidentified flying objects". We
look at such reports solely in the context of our
responsibilities for identifying any potential threat to this
country's security. We consider reports of unexplained aerial
phenomena submitted to us in order to establish whether what
was seen may have been of defence significance. If no threat
is discerned, and this has been case in all such instances to
date, then our interest in the sighting ceases. As such a
sighting can remain unexplained, but not result in any further

official action. We have no role in "UFO" research of the type

alluded to by SERRIZUa.

Gerry Sutcliffe, MP
SEI’ s letter refers to the alleged incident at
Rendlesham Forest/RAF Woodbridge in December 1980. Based on
the available substantiated evidence at the time, a collective
decision by those within the MOD/RAF sections with
responsibility for air defence matters at that time judged that
nothing of defence concern occurred in or around Rendlesham
Forest/RAF Woodbridge on the nights in question. Nothing has
emerged during the last 14 years to make us question that
original judgement. My Department has no knowledge of the
films which claims exist and alleges to be in our

possession.

SOME Makes reference to “UFO" research activity entitled
"Operation Aeneid". Neither I nor my officials are aware of
any such operation under this or any other name. I have
already explained that my Department has no role whatsoever in
"UFO" research and as such my officials are not sent to "UFO"

Conferences.

SESRMEDMENN' s queries in connection with the United States and
South Africa are matters for the Governments of those countries
and should be addressed directly to them. It is not for me to

comment.
I hope this is helpful.

LORD HENLEY

"This office, in consultation with the Department's air defence eXperts,
seeks to determine whether there is any evidence of a breach of thA UK's
air defences" - "I can confirm that to date it remains the case tha
have no evidence to substantiate the existence of craft or lifeforms o
extraterrestrial origin." (EQCOMMMMMN, Secretariat Air Staff 2a MOD,
in a letter dated 8th March 1995 on behalf of Lord Henley)

"What was peculiar about the Ministry of Defence's response was that
they were asked as early as 81. What went on around Rendlesham 7? Were there
not funny things seen on Radar ? Didn't something come down near this
important base ? And they said no no no nothing nothing nothing, and
in effect they lied, which in my day we didn't, we stalled. We refused
to answer questions." (Retired MOD official, speaking about the Rendlesham
forest, Suffolk, UFO incident - December 1980)

"It seems to me that something physical: took place. I have no doubt
that something landed at this U.S, Airforce base and I have no doubt that
it got the people concerned, the U.S. Airforce people and the Commanding
General at the base into a very considerable state” - "Either large numbers
of people, including the Commanding General, were hallucinating, or what
they say happened did happen." (Lord Peter Hill Norton G.C.B., speaking
about the Rendlesham 1980 incident)

Since the 2nd World War at least, military establishments world wide have
been amassing reports and data on UFOs. Pilots, Radar operators, many high
ranking retired milita®y officials, astronauts and thousands of people
the world over, have all testified to the existence of craft observed both
in the air and on the ground. Craft displaying properties and technology
weli outside normal or present day understanding. The Rendlesham incident
is not unique, it is only one of many. There are gross inconsistencies with
Section 40 (MOD) statements, because U.S. military officials have
testified that a craft of unknown origin came down in Rendlesham forest,
late December 1980. A retired MOD official has stated on television that
the MOD have lied about the incident (statement above). British air space
was breached by a craft exhibiting, according to U.S. military officials,
technological properties of a completely unknown nature. The MOD even denied
having received a memo about the incident, written by one of the witnesses,
Lt. Col. Charles Halt USAF. This surfaced under the American freedom
of information act, and the US authorities obtained their copy of the memo
from the British MOD ! .

The world wide quest for the truth about military knowledge on the UFO
issue is gaining momentum, and we invite you to join us. This issue is
not an issue of U.K. national defence, common sense dictates that the MOD
has known this for at least 50 years, ever since pilots filed UFO reports
during the 2nd World War. This quest has nothing to do with believing or
not believing in extraterrestrial intelligence, it is simply a quest for
the truth. We ask that military establishments world wide share with the
people of the world, all they know and understand about UFOs.

You can help this effort by first educating yourself to the subject. Begin
to read the books, go to the conferences, watch the videos and generally
"get on’ the information net work ! Make yourself aware !

JOIN US IN THIS INTERNATIONAL QUEST FOR THE TRUTH
THE ALIEN ACKNOWLEDGMENT CAMPAIGN — OPERATION RIGHT TO KNOW

WE ASK THAT THE MOD TELL THE TRUTH

END UFO SECRECY NOW

REPORT OF AN UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECT

1. Date, Time and See attachoal

Duration of Sighting

2. Description of Object (No of
objects, size, shape, colour,
brightness}

3. Location (indoor/outdoor
stationary/moving)

4. How observed (naked eye,
binoculars, other optical
device, still or moving)

5. Direction in which object first
seen (A landmark may be more
useful than a badly estimated
bearing)

6. Angle of Sight (Estimated
heights are unreliable)

7. Distance (By reterence to
a Known landmark)

8. Movements (Changes in 5,6 & 7
may be of more use than
estimates of course and speed}

9. Met conditions during
observations (Moving cloud,
haze, mist, etc)

10. Nearby objects (Telephone
lines, high voltage lines
reservoir,lake or dam, swamp or
marsh, river, high buildings,
tall chimneys,steeples,spires,
TV or Radio masts, airfields,
generating plant, factories,
pits or other sites with
floodlights or night lighting)

11. To whom reported (Police,
military, press, etc)

Name

12. Name and Address of Informant
Address

13. Background of informant that
may be volunteered

14. Other Witnesses |

15. Date, Time of Receipt |

16. Any Unusual Meteorological
Conditions

17. Remarks

L. Date, Time & Duration of Incident 292000z Jan 95.
2, Description of Incident Heard 8 to 10 bangs/booms on 202000z- but this

morning, 30 Jan, went ourtside and found a piece of
metal on his patio and became very concerned and
thencalled the local Fire Brigade.

3. Location, indoor/outdoor, He was inside
stationary/moving

5. Direction in which noise first heard From the sky

voltage lines, reservoir, lake or dam,
swamp or marsh, river, high buildings,

tall chimneys, steeples, spires, TV or

radio masts, airfields, generating pliant,
factories, pits or other sites with floodlights

or night lighting)

11. To whom reported (Police, military, press etc) Called Fire Brigade who took metal piece
away and an Environmental Officer also
visited.

12. Name & Address of Informant

West Midlands
Section 40)
13. Background of Informant that may be volunteered Used to be in Army, believes that the

piece of metal comes from a satellite
and that it may be contaminated with
radioactivity and that he might now
as as he had picked up the fragment.

14. Other Witmesses Wife - heard the bangs on Sunday evening
Local Fire Brigade - has the piece of metal
Local Environmental Officer - heard his story

15. Date, Time of Receipt (in AFOR) 302025z Jan 95
16. Any Unusual Meteorological Conditions Nil
17. Remarks. Section 40 FEN quite convinced that I was hiding something. He expressed

GREAT concern that the piece of metal could be radioactive and from a satelliteand wished
to be told that it was not. I assured him that I knew nothing about any satellite falling from
the sky but he said that I had to say that with the Official Secrets Act!! Was not really
prepared to believe that the pieceof metal was not dangerous. I told him that I would pass
his account to you and I also gave him BRO tel no.

REPORT OF AN UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECT

1. Date, Time and 26 Jan 95 at 2030
Duration of Sighting 3-4 minutes

2. Description of Object (No of Reddish triangular
objects, size, shape, colour, shaped object with
brightness) lights flashing

3. Location (indoor/outdoor Outdoors
stationary/moving)

4. How observed (naked eye, Naked eye
binoculars, other optical
device, still or moving)

5. Direction in which object first Northerly
seen (A landmark may be more
useful than a badly estimated
bearing)

6. Angle of Sight (Estimated Approx 60°
heights are unreliable)

7. Distance (By reference to Not Known
a known landmark}

8. Movements (Changes in 5,6 & 7 Hovering at first
may be of more use than then in the space of
estimates of course and speed) a second or two,

moved some 2 miles
away on the same
horizontal.

9. Met conditions during Moderately cloudy but
observations (Moving cloud, not adverse
haze, mist, etc)

10. Nearby objects (Telephone Nil
lines, high voltage lines
reservoir,lake or dam, swamp or
marsh, river, high buildings,
tall chimneys,steeples,spires,

TV or Radio masts, airfields,
generating plant, factories,
pits or other sites with
flocdlights or night lighting)

11. To whom reported (Police, MOD AFOR
military, press, etc)

12. Name and Address of Informant
Name
Address
(mobile)
13. Background of informant that Nil
may be volunteered
14. Other Witnesses | None
15. Date, Time of Receipt | 28 1800% Jan
16. Any Unusual Meteorological None
Conditions
17. Remarks The informant was
reluctant to pass
this information on
to us for fear of
ridicule. However
after he saw the
weekend's media
coverage of the BA
pilots who apparently
saw the same object,
felt it was
his duty to
corroborate their
story.
Date: 30 Jan 95 RO2
Duty Operations Officer

Date: 18 January 1995

MINUTE SHEET

Subject Station: PONTEFRACT DIVISION
REPORTED UFO DIVISIONAL OFFICER : PONTEFRACT
SIGHTING

At 1956 hours on Wednesday 18 January 1995 a call was
received at the Wakefield Area Control Room reporting
seeing strange Tights in the sky above Castleford. A
command and contro? Tog was created, number 1728, and
passed to the Pontefract Help Desk for action.

At 2030 hours the same d I telephoned the caller Mr
Secton40 NMS ocon 40 of
Section 20 Tr

About 1900 hours on 18 January 1995 he and his wife
were leaving his mother-in-law's house at
SR, Castleford,

He looked up and saw two groups of lights in diamond
shaped formations above the houses opposite. There
were four or five lights within each group. He says
the lights were bright but not dazzling, like tubes
with diffusers over them.

He states they were travelling fairly Slowly from east
to west, they stopped and the lights seemed to move
about within the diamond formation before moving off
at speed.

The objects gave off a yellow light, appeared round or
oval shaped, and there was no sound associated with

them.

At the time of the sighting the night was clear with
good visibility and the caller estimates his angle of
vision to have been about 60° observed with the naked
eye for five to ten seconds.

The witness is unable to estimate the size of the
lights as he does not know how far away they were from

“PoaMINIC him.

FILE NOTE \ )

1. At 1215 hrs we received a call from who
reported a "UFO" sighting to West Drayton earlier this week;
West Drayton told him to report the sighting to the MOD.

2. At 1705 hrs on Wednesday 21 December in Suffolk outside
his home he saw a very bright light which was at very high
altitude at high speed. It was pulsating and moving in a
zigzig manner; the night was very clear and frosty and the
sighting lasted 5 minutes. BAQQZWMNM's brother also witnessed
the sighting.

3. I advised of our interest in such sightings and
told him that if an explanation was forthcoming, that I would
let him know. ——

4, His details are as follows:

28th November 1994 1700hrs for approx. 3 - 4 minutes

one unidentified ‘lying object described as triangular in shape and of very large size, no distinguishable
colours, having two large bright white lights and the object being surrounded by pulsating red lights.
described as being silent.

firstly observed by informant in his car in Burrs road,Ciacton on sea, then continued to be observed in the
informants garden of his home address at BERCMRAOME Clacton on sea, Essex

observed with the naked eye.

Towards the direction of Thorpe le soken.

approximately 45 degrees from ground

described as being about aeroplane normal flying distance away

described as cruising speed towards the general direction of Thorpe and Colchester.

clear night sky
informant states there was an aeroplane in his sight of vision at the time of observation which he ciaims
was higher in the sky than the u.f.o. but travelling in another direction. There were no other objects nearby
to interfere with the observation.
reported to Clacton Police control,
None known.
Informants mother also witnessed the event ERUOMRAOMEE Of the sane address

Report received at 171 thes 28.1 £.94

Task that this report be forwarded for the attention of A.C.C.(A) for onward transmission to

Tt will be seen trom the [RIS incident 736 of 28.11.94, that objects were seen bv other observers. Police
Ofcersinchided. The crew of ORé|, P's SI cc tey sere
watching a low flying aircratt. RERIQMIOMMEMEN saw an object from a moving vehicle on The Avenue of
Remembrance at Coichester. There is no way of telling if the officers are describing the same object. Mrs
SECs F sex, reported seeing an object at Thorpe le Soken, Essex, which
apparently ties in with the account from the original informant. [ have been unable to recontact
to question her in detail about direction of flight. angle of viewing etc. as the only reply trom her home is an
answerphone.

No enquiries have been made trom this station with any establishments who may have had aircratt in the area at
the time of the sightings, which may offer explanations of what was actually seen.

{ ask that these reports be forwarded via ACC(A), in accordance with Standing Orders, to the Ministry of
Defence.

7. yale

Dear Sir,

Acting on the advice of Beane , author, I am writing
to tell you of a sighting I had last week which he said would

possibly interest you.

My occupation is that of Sql and I am currently
seconded to the BRamel . I work in the Steel in Norwich.

On Tuesday, 15th November, I was in the
Office which is located in the administration building known
as occupies a position on
about a hundred feet above the level of the City. The
building faces W.S.W. directly towards the C.E.Cathedral.

At approximately 4.15 p.m. a member of the Support Staff asked
me whether I could identify a row of bright lights to the West.
Together we looked at a map of the City and conciuded that the
lights were on the ground in the vicinity of the University.
They appeared to be the kind of lights used on a sports ground.
My colleague had not noticed them before.

The mext day, Wednesday, I was in the office, the time being
approximately 7.45 a.m. I looked once again at the map of the
City and having done so went to the window to see whether I
could identify where it was that we had seen the lights on the
previous day.

The morning was exceptionally bright, the sky clear of cloud.
The air temperature was mild, there was little, if any, breeze.
There was no haze over the City.

While I was searching the sky-line to the right, or W.N.W., my
eyes were made conscious of a light to my left, two lights in
fact. I enclose a sketch I made a couple of minutes later which
will give an idea of the position of the images in relation to
the spire of the Cathedral. The sketch is made sight-size.

At first I thought the scene was ordinary enough; the rising
sun was already strong and it seemed that I was seeing two hot

air balloons, formed from a silvery material. They were identical

lies

in size and perfectly round, about 1/4" to 3/8" diameter.

They appeared solid, faced directly to the sun I neither
expected to see nor did I see any lessening of reflected

light to the edges of the images and so they appeared not to
be spherical. Neither of the images showed any movement during
the minute or so that I observed them.

I noticed that there were no baskets beneath the balloons and
although they appeared to have little altitude I saw no bursts
of flame as I should have expected. The brightness of the
light coming from the surfaces of the objects made it
impossible for me to estimate their distance.

There was neither sight nor sound of aircraft, neither was
there, after the images ceased to be visible, any cloud or
indication of their presence. The objects appeared to be
substantial, to be reflecting light and not emanating it.

Without the objects having moved in relation to the buildings
beneath them, after about a minute they both vanished in an
instant; they did diminish in intensity of light or size but
vanished from view. ner

The windows to the office had been cleaned two or three days
before the sighting. There was no light on in the office and
neither was any of the office equipment switched on. What I

saw was not a reflection on the window from within the room.

The sketch was made immediately after I saw the images and
drawn from the same position in which I had stood. At no time
while watching the images or objects did they make any kind of
movement.

If I am able to help you in any way in connection with the

matter described will you please contact me. If you need to do
so by telephone please will you do so after 7.00 p.m.

Yours faithfully,

useful than a badly estimated

a Known landmark)

may be of more use than movements, appeared
estimates of course and speed) to be searching for a
